2037. THOMAS HAMILTON and ANN HAMILTON were indicted for stealing, on the 26th of September, 20lbs. weight of chrystal, value 3l., the goods of Israel Barnett.
MR. CLARKSON conducted the prosecution.
ISRAEL BARNETT. I live in Hungerford-street, and deal in minerals - the male prisoner was in my service. On the 26th of September, between seven and eight o'clock in the morning, the female prisoner and some chrystal were brought to my house by the officer; the male prisoner was at work there at the time - the officer brought a basket of chrystals, and put them on the board; I heard the male prisoner say, "There are some officers with my wife" - I was then called by my servant, and went down - the officer asked where I kept my chrystal; I said in the top back room, and the male prisoner worked in the top front room - there is a passage and stair-case between the rooms; there are leads to the house, but he would have no business on them - in July last I picked out some chrystals, and put them into the top back room; there were some particular pieces, which I knew, and in those which the officer brought back were some of those pieces - I had never given any to the prisoner nor sold him any, nor authorized him to sell any; the whole of this chrystal belongs to myself - there is about 20lbs.; it cost me about 3l.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q.What are you? A. I deal and work in chrystals, minerals, and all kinds of stones, and am a butcher - I only keep the male prisoner, and a man who carries out the meat; I employ other men out of doors - the male prisoner was employed in cutting chrystals, and had been with me off and on about seven years; he worked in the front room next the street - I do not know whether a man, who wanted to know what o'clock it was, could see from the front room, but he might from the leads very easily; I do not know whether he was employed by any other person, or dealt in chrystals himself - I believe all these were part of mystock, but I have no mark on them; I know this piece to be mine, by having it several times in my hand - I have perhaps 2000 lbs. weight; my daughter and wife give out the work - I had seen this piece on the Thursday; the female prisoner was brought on the Saturday.
MR. CLARKSON. Q.When the woman was brought in, did you examine your stock of chrystals? A. No, but I did when the male prisoner ran away, and missed a considerable number.
COURT. Q. I see part of this is clear, and the other part is not? A. Yes, I knocked a piece off, to see if it was clear, to go to work.
WILLIAM FORTY. I am a Thames Police-surveyor. On Saturday morning, the 26th of September, I was in Craven-street, and saw the female prisoner with this basket of chrystals; I asked how she came by it - she said she brought it from home, and was to have met her husband at Charing-cross, but, as he had not come, she was going back with it; I left her in charge of an officer, made inquiries, and took her to Mr. Barnett's house - in consequence of what I said, the male prisoner came down; I asked if he had given his wife any chrystal - he said Yes, he had half an hour before, and he would put on his coat and explain it to me; he went up stairs - I followed him a few steps, and he said," Come on;" I thought it was all right, and waited for him - he made his escape at the top of the house; I went up to the top of the house, and saw some saw-dust, and a foot-mark in the gutter - a person might get from the workshop window, to the window of the room where the chrystal is kept; the rooms are on the same floor.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q.What time did you meet her? A.Between seven and eight o'clock in the morning; I do not know that the prisoner was afraid of a civil process for debt.
MR. CLARKSON. Q.When he talked about his coat he did not say any thing about a civil process? A. No.
ESTHER BARNETT. I am the prosecutor's wife. I know these two pieces of chrystal - I know this one by its having a rough coat, and by its form; it is my husband's - they were among our stock of chrystals; I had seen them on the Friday.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. I suppose if you had seen them in Whitechapel, or any where, you would have known them again? A. Yes, they have been so often in my hands; I do not know that there ever were such pieces any where else - (looking at some other pieces produced by the prisoners' Counsel) I know some of these to be ours; this one by a flaw, and this by its being partly coloured.
HANNAH BARNETT. I am the prosecutor's daughter. I know these two pieces, and know this piece; I had seen it on the Friday morning, and washed it.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Do you know this piece? A. Yes - these two pieces I had given to the prisoner; this other I know by a piece being knocked off by a chisel.
THOMAS DOWN. I was in the prosecutor's service. Hamilton has told me he could see what o'clock it was on the leads - I have seen him get out of the window; St. Martin's clock cannot be seen from the front room - it lays at the back of the house; I have seen him get up at the window about twice a day.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. How long did he stay? A.Perhaps two minutes, or a minute - he was in my sight while he was in the room.
THOMAS FOGG. I am an officer. I took the male prisoner on the 28th of September, on Tower-hill - I told him it was on suspicion of a felony at Mr. Barnett's; he said he was going to Mr. Barnett's.
Thomas Hamilton's Defence. No person can go from the top of the house to the back premises - his daughter has sent me out to see the time; Down stood by me all day at work - chrystal is a thing no man can swear to; I escaped because I was in debt.
WILLIAM SMITH. I am a lapidary, and live in Goswell-street. I have been in the business fifty years - I know the nature of chrystal; it is not uncommon for it to have a rough coat - I could not swear to chrystal, nor can any man; I gave half a dozen pieces of chrystal to this gentleman, but I could not identify them after they are out of my sight; I had these pieces ever since May last.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Had you ever bought chrystal of the prisoner? A. I bought some in a worked state eight or nine months ago, which he told me he bought of Mr. Cashmore.
COURT. Q. Did you know that at that time he was in the employ of a dealer in chrystals? A. He told me that Mr. Barnett had not employ for him, and he had bought some chrystals.
JAMES COX. I am an optician, and live in Barbican. I have dealt in chrystals all my life - it is impossible to swear to them without having a private mark; I have bought chrystals of the prisoner.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18291029-196]
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
2038. JANE BAIN was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of September, 49 prints, value 40s., the goods of Henry Triggs; and ROBERT SIMPSON was indicted for feloniously receiving part of the said prints, well knowing them to have been stolen.
TWO OTHER COUNTS, stating them to be the goods of Thomas Norton Longman and other persons.
MESSRS. BOLLAND and CRESWELL conducted the prosecution.
HENRY TRIGGS. I am a copper-plate printer, employed by Messrs. Longman, Rees and Co., Westley and Co., and Mr. Marshall, to take impressions from steel plates for the forthcoming annuals, the Friendship's Offering, the Gem, the Literary Souvenir, and the Juvenile Forget Me Not; I was employed first about June last - the respective proprietors find paper for those plates: I had struck off impressions from them all, previous to the 19th of September, but never gave any of them out of my custody. The female prisoner was in my employ, as servant, and had been so about six weeks previous to her apprehension - I gave her a positive injunction when she came into my service not to touch the prints; on the 18th or 19th of September she was taken into custody - it was on a Thursday; I accused her of stealing the prints - she said she was very sorry, that she had given them to her mother for her youngest brother, and her mother was a very wicked bad woman to sell them - I do not recollect her saying how many she had taken.
COURT. Q. Did you say they had been sold? A. I said she had almost ruined me by giving these prints to her mother, and their being exposed for public sale, as it would show I was not a trustworthy person; I could not ascertain how many I had lost - we have an immense quantity, and it would be impossible to detect one or two dozen - at that time none ought to have been issued to the public - I had delivered some of the Juvenile Forget Me Not to Messrs. Westley and Davis.
MR. CRESWELL. Q. Had you any right to any of them yourself? A. According to the custom of the trade we consider ourselves entitled to one or two impressions, not more.
Cross-examined by MR. LEE. Q. I believe you were suspected of having parted with them? A. Yes, at first: after the examination at the office I asked Simpson how he came by the prints, and he said an elderly woman with a child sold them to him - he asked me if there was any one I suspected about my house; I said I could not possibly suspect any one of my work-people; at my suggestion he went willingly to my house, to see if he could point out the person who sold them - he said it was not my female servant; it struck me it might probably be her mother - we went to her mother's in London-street; he knocked, and I staid outside - her mother came to the door, and he told me that was the woman; I had parted with some of them to Messrs. Westley's binders, to be bound in the usual way, and I think to some others. Simpson was out on bail; I believe there were some impressions exhibited by the resective proprietors.
HENRY BLOMFIELD. I am in the employ of Thomas Norton Longman and others. I went to Simpson's house, in Tottenham-court-road; he deals in books and prints - this was on the 19th of September; I saw him, and asked for some prints - he produced several portfolios, and I saw some impressions for the Gem and the Literary Souvenir for the coming year; the publishers of those works get them out about the end of October - I bought two of them at 6d. each, and asked if he had any more; he said he had had more, and might possibly have some in a week or two- I marked the two I bought - these are them; these letters at the bottom of the print are not put by the engraver, but by a person he sends them to - we receive them perfect - this one is "Jacob's Dream," published by Longman and Co., and this is
"Saturday Night," published by Marshall.
Cross-examined by MR. LEE. Q. When you went to the shop did you see any other prints of a similar description? A. No; I saw none in the window - I went into the back shop: they were in the port-folios, among others - no doubt persons buy a great many prints, and sell one print for more than they give for the whole; these are in the same state as I bought them, except my marking - one of them was soiled; when they are soiled they are of little value.
COURT. Q. What would the value of them be if not soiled? A.Perhaps 6d. or 1s., or 1s. 6d.; Simpson sells second-hand books - these could not be the engraver's proofs, because they have the letters to them.
WILLIAM RUDOLPH. I am in the service of Joseph Hogarth, who lives in Somer's-terrace; I went to Mr. Simpson's, at the corner of Howland-street, Tottenham-court-road: I asked him if he had any engravings for the annuals - he said he had, and I bought five of him; these are them - here is our "Holyday Time," two of "Saturday Night," one of "The Stolen Interview," and one "The Masquerade;" they have the letters to them - he took them from a shelf in the shop.
Cross-examined. Q. I suppose he sold them in the usual way? A. Yes; I did not look whether any were exhibited about the wall or the window.
JOSEPH HOGARTH. I am a print colourer. On the 19th of September I went to Mr. Simpson's shop, and bought five prints of annuals and another; these are them - some of them are good prints: there may be one or two damaged or dirty - I gave 2s. for the five; I asked where he got them - he said from a girl who represented that she had been sent by her young mistress, and he supposed they were engravers' proofs.
Cross-examined Q. Are they precisely in the same state as when you bought them? A. Yes, with the exception of my mark - it frequently happens that prints of considerable value at one time, are sold at another, at a very small price.
WILLIAM MARSHALL. I am a bookseller, and live in Middle-row. I publish the Gem; I gave the steel plates to Mr. Triggs, to have engravings struck off. On the 21st of September I went to Simpson's house, and bought three or four prints, for which I gave 6d. each - these are them - I did not see Simpson; these prints have been rumpled a little.
Cross-examined. Q. Where did you get them? A. This one from the window, and some of them from a portfolio; I was in the back shop - persons passing by might have seen this one in the window. The Gem has twelve prints in it, and we sell the book at 12s. - we charge the trade 7s. for the set.
MR. BOLLAND. Q. Do the prints vary in value? A. Yes; the plates cost from one hundred to one hundred and eighty guineas a piece - these prints are worth 1s. or 1s. 6d. each.
THOMAS ANDREWS. I am in the employ of Frederick Westley and Davis - they live in Stationer's-court, and are the publishers of the Juvenile Forget Me Not, and are part proprietors. I went to Mr. Simpson's on the 22nd of September - I saw him, and asked if he had any prints for a scrap-book; he said, "Yes, walk in" and shewed me two,"The Schoolmaster" and "The Masquerade;" I bought"The Schoolmaster" for 7d. - he had asked 8d. for it; I offered him 6d. - he said he could not take less than 8d., unless I took a number of them, as they were prints not yet published, and he had to pay a good price for them; he said some persons bought the books, and cut the plates out, but they got theirs in a different way - these are the prints.
Cross-examined. Q. From where were they produced? A. From a shelf in the back shop - he did not state that he got them at a public auction; I did not ask where he got them.
HENRY RICHARD BUCKERIDGE. On the 23rd of September I went with a search-warrant, and took Simpson -I got five prints, which I left with Hewitt.
Cross-examined. Q.Where were they? A.Three of them in the window, and two on a shelf; he said I might search, and did not attempt to escape.
JANE BAIN. I married the female prisoner's father, and live at No.30, London-street, Tottenham-court-road; she brought me some prints about the 15th of September: I did not count how many - there might be a dozen and a half or two dozen; she said she brought them for Tommy, a little boy, I have to nurse - I put them into a press, and kept them till the Thursday or Friday, and being very much distressed for money to buy food, I went to the corner of Howland-street, and saw Mr. Simpson - I asked if he would have the goodness to buy these prints; he said they were of little or no value to him, and he did not care about purchasing them; I begged of him to purchase them, and said I wanted 6d., or at least a few pence - he gave me 6d.; I think these are the prints - they were this kind; I begged him to give me two back, which he did, and they were taken out of my press by the officer; there was a gentleman in Mr. Simpson's parlour at the time - he did ask how I came by them; I said my husband's daughter had given them to me, and I supposed her mistress or master had given them to her.
Cross-examined. Q. I believe she gave them to you for the child? A. Yes, but I had not given them to the child; Mr. Simpson did appear to be engaged, and refused to take them at all, but by my begging him he took them, and gave me two back; I believe there was only one person in his shop - he just pulled them asunder, and looked at them; he seemed anxious to go into the back shop.
JAMES BRADSHAW. I am a constable. I went with Mr. Simpson to the last witness; I found these two prints there - I searched the box of the female prisoner at Mr. Triggs', and found these twenty-six prints there - Mr. Triggs was present.
Cross-examined. Q. It was through Simpson you found her? A. Yes; he said he had found the old wretch who sold them.
CHARLOTTE TRIGGS. I am the wife of Henry Triggs; I was present when the female prisoner's box was searched - it was in the same state that it had been before.
HENRY TRIGGS. To the best of my belief these prints were all under my care, and were mine.
Prisoner Bain. I leave it to the mercy of the Jury.
Simpson's Defence. I leave it entirely to my Counsel.
BAIN - GUILTY. Aged 37.
Confined Six Weeks.
SIMPSON - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18291029-197]
2039. WILLIAM DAVIS was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of October, 2 live pigs, price 18s., the property of James West.
JAMES WEST. I live in Camden-street, Bethnal-green, and keep pigs - the sty is not shut; they run in my yard: they were safe at eleven o'clock on the 11th of October, and at five o'clock the next morning I missed them; I saw them afterwards at the green-yard, and knew them.
WILLIAM WARD. I received information on the 18th of October, and went to the prisoner's house, No. 8, North-street; he was at home, and two pigs were running in the back room - he said he had bought those two honestly, and he could bring the person forward of whom he bought them - I was at that time looking for a large sow; the owner was with me, and he said, "I think there is something concealed under the floor;" the prisoner said No - we then pulled out a nail and a gimblet, and opened a small door, which led to a kind of cave under the house - we then opened another door, and found the sow, which the person who was with me claimed; West has seen the two pigs, and claimed them.
JOHN PURSER. On the 16th of October I lost a large sow; I went on the 18th, with Ward, to the prisoner's house, and saw the two pigs, which the prisoner said he had bought honestly.
JAMES PARKER. I am a constable. I went to the prisoner's house - what has been stated is correct.
GEORGE ALLWORTHY. There were five pigs and a sow driven to my house on the 18th of October; the prosecutor came and owned two of them; I keep the greenyard.
SAMUEL HARMER. I am a shoemaker. On the 18th of October I went to the prisoner's house, and saw the two pigs and the sow.
Prisoner's Defence I bought the two small pigs, and gave 18s. for them - the sow walked in, and I could not get her out; she walked into the cellar.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
2040. WILLIAM DAVIS was again indicted for stealing, on the 18th of October, 1 sow, price 50s., the property of John Purser.
JOHN PURSER. I live in James-street, Globe-fields. -On the 16th of October my sow ran out of my yard, when the gate was open for a cart to go in. I went on the 18th to the prisoner's house, and saw two pigs in the back room - under the boards I found my sow; the prisoner said he had no such thing there, and had I not had a little bit of an item I should never have found it - it was a very secret place.
SAMUEL HARMER. I was present - what has been stated is correct.
Prisoner's Defence. I am as innocent as a new born babe; I opened the door, and shewed him the sow - she ran into my house, flew at me, and went into the cellar.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years for each offence.
[trialAccount t18291029-198]
2041. WILLIAM PULLEN was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of October, 3 live pigs, price 30s., the property of Mary Harmer.
SAMUEL HARMER. I am the son of Mary Harmer - she lives in Ann-street, Globe-fields; she lost five pigs out of her yard, and three of them were found on the 18th; I went that day to the prisoner, who lives a few doors from Davis, the last prisoner; I told him I had lost some pigs - he said he had two, and after some altercation he let me in, and shewed me one, which was one of my mother's; I then went on to Davis, and this prisoner either came or was brought there - I then said, "Perhaps Pullen will let me go back to his house" - he said Yes; we went back, and I saw my mother's three pigs there, in a temporary sty, but I think they must have run from under the boards; it was like magic to me - I said, "As I have found them I shall take you into custody;" they had been lost at two separate times, on the 14th and 16th.
WILLIAM WARD. I was present; the pigs were found just as the witness has described - I saw them come from a sort of concealed place under the boards.
JOHN PURSER. I was present when the pigs came from the cellar, and there were a number of tools found, which a poor man had lost.
Prisoner's Defence. I did not take them with an intent of stealing them - they came into my house, and I kept them in my back room; they came at half-past six o'clock in the evening, while I went to get a pail of water - I inquired if any body had lost them, and could not find any one who had; on the Sunday the prosecutor came, and I shewed him one - he said it was not his: he went to Davis', and I followed him - the officer, who was there, asked him if he had seen his pigs; he said No - I said I had another pig; he went to see it, and then identified them.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18291029-199]
2042. ANN WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of October, 3 half-sovereigns, 3 half-crowns, 5 shillings, and 4 sixpences, the monies of James Gravenor.
JAMES GRAVENOR. I am a weaver. On the 17th of October, between one and two o'clock in the day, I met the prisoner and another in Whitechapel - I was sober; they asked me to treat them with something to drink - we went to a wine-vaults at the corner of George-street, and had a quartern of gin; they then invited me to go home -I went home with the other girl, not the prisoner; we went up stairs into a room - I had then three half-sovereigns, three half-crowns, and some shillings, which I shifted into my coat pocket, and pulled my coat off; the prisoner was not there then, but in a few minutes I turned round, and saw the prisoner with my coat in her hand, going down stairs - I went to go after her, and the other girl who was in the room took hold of me, shut the door, and kept me till the prisoner got out; I then got down, and went into the street in my shirt-sleeves - I went about twenty yards; the prisoner called me, put my coat on the step of a door, and ran away laughing; I took the coat, and missed all the money but 6d.; I went to Lambeth-street, described her, and she was taken that day week - I am positive she is the girl; I have not got any of the money.
THOMAS GOODING. I am an officer. Gravenor de-described the prisoner to me; I had seen her about Whitechapel before - I met her in Shoreditch, and took her to the office; I put her with several women, and the prosecutor pointed her out - I had looked for her where I had been accustomed to see her, and could not find her.
Prisoner's Defence. I had only just come out of fourteen days' imprisonment, and had not been in Whitechapel at all.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18291029-200]
2043. GEORGE PALMER was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of October, 1 handkerchief, value 5s., the goods of George William Jennings, from his person.
MR. GEORGE WILLIAM JENNINGS. I am of no profession. I was going to the Haymarket-theatre on the 15th of October, with two friends - the constable came up to me, and asked if I had lost a handkerchief; I felt, and said I had: he said, "Is this yours?" I said, "It is certainly the colour of mine, but I cannot be certain of it."
JESSE PHILLIPS. I am a parish-constable. On the 15th of October I saw the prisoner, whom I knew, and another person with him - they followed the prosecutor and two gentlemen who were with him for some distance; the prisoner took this yellow handkerchief from the prosecutor's pocket, and was putting it into his flap; he came round the coach-stand, and I took him; I went to the prosecutor, who said he had lost a handkerchief of this description.
WILLIAM WHITTINGHAM. I am a commission-agent. I saw the prisoner take the handkerchief from the prosecutor; I took it off the ground.
Prisoner's Defence. It is my own - I took it out of my pocket, and used it - the officer took me; they said they would not take me to Bow-street, or they should get blown up.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18291029-201]
2044. WILLIAM WEST and GEORGE LILLEY were indicted for stealing, on the 10th of September, 1 pair of shoes, value 10s., the goods of John Creasey.
JOHN CREASEY. I am a shoemaker, and live in Seymour-street. On the 10th of September I left my boy in the shop - while I was out these shoes were taken.
FRANCIS KEYS. On the 10th of September I was with Rierdon - we watched the two prisoners for a few minutes - they stopped at a pawnbroker's; West left the other, and went into the shop - I followed him, and saw him with these shoes on the counter; I went to the door, and told Rierdon to go on - he went and took Lilley, who was then going away; I went and asked West where he got the shoes - he said a young man at the door gave them to him to pawn; I asked if he ever saw that young man before - he said No: I took him to the door, and said, "Is this the young man?" he said No; I said,"I saw you in conversation with him" - I took them to the office; I did not know who they belonged to, but I saw the name of E. Driver, Esq. on them, looked in the Court Guide, and found him.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. You saw the shoes in the shop? A. Yes - that was the first I saw of them; he denied that Lilley was the person who gave them to him.
DANIEL RIERDON. I was outside, and took Lilley - I found nothing on him.
West's Defence. There was nothing but "Driver" on the shoes; I told the officer the person was waiting at the corner of the street, and if he would allow me to go to the corner I should be able to clear myself - he said, "No, I have got you;" in going to the watch-house he said,"Why don't you, like a good lad, say he gave them to you?" I said, "I will not bring an innocent lad into trouble;" the person asked me where the nearest pawnbroker's was - I said I would pawn them; he said pawn them for 5s. in the name of William Ring, Pancras-street.
JURY to FRANCIS KEYS. Q. Did he ask you to go to Pancras-street? A. Yes, but I did not, because I had seen him with the other.
WEST - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
LILLEY - NOT GUILTY.

2045. WILLIAM COLLINS was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of October, 1 ham, value 10s., the goods of Anthony Medcalf; and that, at the General Session for thedelivery of the Gaol of Newgate, held on Thursday, the 31st of May, in the 8th year of his present Majesty's Reign, he was convicted of felony, by the name of William Davies .

ANTHONY MEDCALF. This is my ham here.

2046. JAMES CARTWRIGHT was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of October, 1 trowel, value 2s., the goods of Joseph Taylor; and 1 trowel, value 1s., the goods of Thomas Taylor; and that he was in July, in the 8th year of his present Majesty's Reign, convicted of felony.
JOSEPH TAYLOR. I am a bricklayer, and was working at an unfinished building in Percy-street, Kingsland-road. On the 16th of October, when I went to dinner, I left my trowel concealed in a new drain in the back yard - the prisoner had no right there; when I returned I missed it - I went to several pawnbrokers and could not find it; I then went to Mr. Thimbleby's, and the prisoner was there offering it to pawn.
JOHN WILLIAM WINTLE. I am an officer. I took the prisoner - this is the trowel.
HENRY FREAK. I live with Mr. Thimbleby. The prisoner brought this trowel, and pawned it on the 16th of October: he came again and brought this other; one the prosecutor came in and claimed it.
THOMAS TAYLOR. I am a bricklayer, and was at work at the same place. This is my trowel, which I lost from there.
RICHARD CONSTANTINE. I am a constable. I have a certificate of the prisoner's conviction in this Court in July, by the name of James Bates; I took the prisoner, and know he is the person - he was ordered to be whipped and discharged.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Transported for Life.

2047. SARAH ELIZA TATTON was indicted for bigamy.
ANN TATTON. I have a son named William Tatton - he was married to the prisoner about seven years ago, on the 28th of August, at St. Mary, Lambeth; she was married, as a widow, in the name of Sarah Eliza Webb - I do not know what she was, but I believe she took in needle-work; my son was a porter - he had been acquainted with her four or five months; she had one child five years old when she married - they had no children; I do not know whether they separated by mutual consent, but he allowed her 5s. a week after they had lived together two years; I have a copy of the register, which I got from the church myself on the 3rd of July, 1828; the reason I have kept it so long is, that she was in prison, and denied knowing such a person as William Tatton; I was present at the marriage (certificate read).
Cross-examined by J. ALLEY. Q. Who did you get the certificate from? A. From the minister himself - his name is on it; my son went to sea three years ago - he was gone fourteen months, and returned in the Ganges; the prisoner did not tell me after he was gone, hat she found a letter of his, saying he had left her for ever - I do not know whether he allowed her any support while he has been away the last three years, but the first voyage he did - he is here now.
BONDFIELD BRYANT. I am a carpenter. I married the prisoner at St. Mary, Islington, on the 2nd of October, 1827 - she represented herself as a widow, by the name of Sarah Webb; I heard Mr. Webb was dead - I did not hear of the other husband; that she stated she had plenty of furniture, but I found none at all.
WILLIAM MORGAN. I am a Police-officer. I have the certificate of the two marriages; I took the prisoner at her brothers's house on the other side of Stepney church (certificate read).
GUILTY. Aged 33.
Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18291029-205]
2048. SAMUEL ADAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of October, 1 coat, value 12s.; 1 hat, value 8s.; 1 snuff-box, value 1s., and 1 pocket-book, value 1s., the goods of James Collins.
ABRAHAM BARKER. I keep the Bacchus, public-house at Hoxton. I was in the skittle-ground about five or six weeks back; Mr. Collins had been there two or three times; and that day he pulled off his coat and hat, left them in the Dutchpin box, and went into another ground; I heard a person had come over the wall; I went out and saw the prisoner; he gave these things into my arms and ran away; I had known him very well by his coming to the ground.
Prisoner. Q. What time was it? A. From half-past five to half-past six o'clock; it was between the lights - you threw the things into my arms - I did not stay three seconds; you are the person who gave them to me - I took them back to Mr. Collins; I did not see you again till you were at the office - it might be four or five weeks afterwards.
JAMES COLLINS. I was at the skittle-ground on the 14th of October, and took off my coat and hat in the Dutchpin ground - I went to another ground, and heard a person drop off the wall on the other side; I looked, and missed my things - I had not seen the prisoner then, but Barker gave me my things; the coat had the pocket-book and snuff-box in the pocket.
JOHN FRYER. The prisoner was described to me; I found him on the 15th of October in Hoxton - he ran away, and was stopped by another person; when I came up, he said, "Do you want me for the Bacchus job?" I said Yes.
Prisoner's Defence. I believe another officer can prove to the contrary; I never was in the skittle-ground that night - do you think, when he saw me only three seconds, and did not see me again for five weeks, he could know me again.?
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Confined Nine Months,
[trialAccount t18291029-206]
2049. MARGARET BURNS and WILLIAM BURNS were indicted for stealing, on the 13th of October, 3 pairs of shoes, value 7s., the goods of James Peart.
FRANCIS KEYS. I am a Bow-street patrol. On the 13th of October I saw the two prisoners near Peart's shop; I saw the woman go to the shop door with a knife, cut a string, and take down a pair of shoes - I went and took her, and found from sixty to one hundred duplicates on her, some for the prosecutor's property.
THOMAS SEABROOK. I am a pawnbroker. I received two pairs of shoes from the female prisoner and her son, on the 29th of December and on the 23rd of September; the last were pawned by the woman - I cannot say who pawned the first.
DAVID JAMES. I am in the service of Mr. James Peart, of Whitechapel. These are all his property.
Margaret Burns ' Defence. I beg for mercy; my child is innocent - I have four children, and nothing to give them to eat.
MARGARET BURNS - GUILTY. Aged 38.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury. - Confined 14 Days.

2050. ALEXANDER JONES and THOMAS TOMPKINS were indicted for stealing, on the 7th of October, 1 pair of trousers, value 12s., the goods of William Powell.
GEORGE FROST. I live with William Powell, a tailor, in Shoreditch. On the 7th of October, between eight and ten o'clock he lost pair of trousers - they had been hanging inside his shop - a woman came in, and said a boy had run away with them; the officer came in a little after eleven, and asked if we had lost a pair of trousers.
THOMAS SHELSWELL. On the 7th of October, about half-past ten o'clock in the forenoon, I was in Petticoat-lane with Lea; I saw the two prisoners together - one of them knew me and he hung back, but Jones came forward, and I saw he had something under his jacket; I stopped him, and told Lea to take the other - I asked Jones what he had got - he first of all said Nothing; I said, "It is rather bulky;" he then said, "A pair of trousers; "I said, "When did you get them;" he said either six weeks or six months ago, he bought them at Portsmouth; I took him into a public-house, and asked if there was any mark on them - he said No, not that he knew of; I found this mark on them, which led me to the prosecutor.
JAMES LEA. I am an officer, and took Tompkins.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Jones' Defence. A man asked me to buy them, and I gave 18s. for them; he said he brought them from Portsmouth six months ago.
JONES - GUILTY. Aged 21.
Confined Three Months.
TOMPKINS - NOT GUILTY.

2051. CATHERINE CONNOR was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of October, 1 gown, value 1s. 6d.; 1 petticoat, value 1s.; 1 pair of stockings, value 3d.; 1 shawl, value 3d., and 1 hat, value 3s., the goods of Bridget Kelly, widow.
BRIDGET KELLY. I am a widow, and live in White Horse-alley, Cow-cross. I have known the prisoner for several years; I let her sleep in my room on the 22d of October - I had not seen her for a long time; when she was gone I missed this property.
JOHN RICHMOND. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, in Christ Church-passage on the 22nd of October; she confessed she had robbed the woman - she had been to the overseer of the parish; I took her to the Compter, and found this shawl, shoes, stockings and the woman's cap on her, and these three duplicates.
GEORGE KNAPP. I am a pawnbroker. I have a bonnet pawned on the 22nd of October - I gave this duplicate for it.
RICHARD EDWARD BARBER. I have a petticoat pawned in the name of Ann Connor - I cannot swear by whom.
JOSEPH COLKETT. I am a pawnbroker. I have a gown pawned by the prisoner.
BRIDGET KELLY. These are my property; I was out at work, and when I came home they were gone.
Prisoner's Defence. I took the gown to take my shoes out of pawn - she had lent me these shoes till I got my own.
GUILTY. Aged 54.

2053. GEORGE HILL was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of October, 1 pair of boots, value 5s., the goods of George Pitt.
ELIZABETH PITT. I am the wife of George Pitt, a shoemaker of Camden-town. On the 8th of October, about eleven o'clock in the morning, I was sitting behind the counter - the prisoner came, took these boots down, and looked at them as if he was going to purchase; he then looked up and down the street, folded them up, and walked off with them - I ran out, and cried Stop thief! I followed him till he was taken; he felt down on a heap of dirt, and then left the boots.

2054. JOHN JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of September, 1 pair of clogs, value 5s., the goods f John Hudson.

Third Middlesex Jury - Before Mr. Common Sergeant.
2055. WILLIAM WALTHAM and JOSEPH PEACH were indicated for stealing, on the 25th of September, 5 bushels of wheat, value 35s., and 2 sacks, value 3s., the goods of Edward Whalley; and ELIZABETH PIGGOTT was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, knowing them to have been stolen.
MR. BODKIN, who conducted the prosecution, declined offering any evidence against Elizabeth Piggott, who was here acquitted.
EDWARD WHALLEY. I am a farmer, and live at South Mimms. On the 25th of September I had fifteen loads of wheat in my barn, which had been dressed for market - there were some sacks of mine there; I saw the place secure at five o'clock - it was locked; I have seen some corn and sacks in the officer's hands; the sacks have my mark on them.
JOHN SIMKINS. I am in Mr. Whalley's employ. I went to the barn on the 26th, in the morning - the door was open, and between five and six bushels of corn taken away, and two sacks; I have seen the sacks since, and knew them, and some corn which seemed to be the same.
BENJAMIN LAMBERT. I am a smith. I was at South Mimms on Saturday, the 26th of September; I saw Waltham, Propstring, the officer, and Mr. Watch, at the Cross Keys - they then went to the cage. and left Waltham there for breaking Mr. Alderman Heygate's pales; I afterwards let Waltham out, and went with him to the house where Elizabeth Piggott and he lived together - Peach was there and Thomas, who is gone away; we had some gin, and all drank - then Peach and Thomas said they would go away; they packed up their clothes and went - I was coming out, and Waltham asked me to stop; I being tipsy did not stop - he said, "I have some wheat I want to move that came from Mr. Whalley's on Friday night;" and by his persuasion I helped to move it in a sack, that Piggott had in the house with some gleaning corn in it - I heard nothing about any other sacks; they said Peach and Thomas had brought it in two sacks - we took it across the road, and put it into Whittington's pond: the sack was taken back to the house - Piggott said they did it for fear they should get into trouble.
Prisoner Waltham. Q. Did not you come to the cage to me, and ask if I would come out? A.No.
WILLIAM HIGGS. I am an officer. I went to Whittington's pond on the 27th of September; we found a great deal of wheat on the bridge and fence, and some in the pond - I found these two sacks under an arch, about a mile and a half from South Mimms; Waltham was in the cage, and the lock was broken: but he came back into the cage himself, as I was told - I went to him; he was quite tipsy - I asked who broke the lock of the cage; he said he could not tell - I went and took Peach at Biggleswade, at work at a blacksmith's shop; I said, "You are my prisoner" - he said, "Don't say any thing about it before these people; say it is for a little one;" and I said so - when we got out I told him it was for taking the wheat from Mr. Whalley's, and told him it would be better to speak the truth.
ELIZABETH PIGGOTT. I was living at South Mimms. There was something brought to my house, but I cannot say what it was, nor who brought it - I was not at home.
COURT. Q. Was there a sack brought in? A. Yes, two - Peach and Thomas were there, but I cannot say whether they came before or afterwards, nor what time of night it was.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18291029-213]
2056. DAVID LYONS was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of October, 6 knives, value 6s., the goods of Thomas Saunders.
THOMAS SAUNDERS. I am a watchmaker, and keep a shop in Whitechapel-road. On the 6th of October, between seven and eight o'clock in the morning, I was standin the parlour, and saw the prisoner with his hand in a little glass-case in the shop - he took out six knives; he came in from the street - I did not know him before; I ran to the door - he was taken by an officer, and brought back; the tray was deficient of six knives.
Cross-examined by MR. LEE. Q. Will you take upon yourself to swear the prisoner's arm was in the glass-case? A. Yes; I was six or eight feet from the place -I saw the prisoner in the shop, and he had his arms in the glass-case; he saw me, drew his arm back, and ran away - the prisoner is the person who took the knives - there were only this half dozen pen-knives; they were in the glass the night before, and the shop had not been opened, only the door - they were dropped at my door, and brought into my shop immediately; I never said the arm I saw in the glass-case I thought was my son's - I said I thought my son was in the shop, as I had just opened my street door, and then went into the parlour; at first I thought it was my son, but when I went to the door I saw she prisoner running - I was as quick in getting to the door as he was; it is a wide road.
ROBERT ROBERTS. I was in Whitechapel-road on the 6th of October, early in the morning; I saw the prisoner running, and some persons after him - I saw Mr. Thompson stop him; I saw some knives in the road a minute or two after I saw the prisoner in the - they were place over which he had run - I did not take them up till he was stopped.
Cross-examined. Q. Were there a great many people? A. No, only one or two running after him - he did not turn any corners.
ANTHONY THOMPSON. I saw the prisoner running, and secured him eighty or a hundred yards from the shop - he turned no corner; the knives were brought into the shop.
Prisoner's Defence. My master sent me to a marine-store shop in the Dog-row, and told me to make haste - I was running, the witness came, and took hold of me; Mr. Thompson took me back, and began searching me - he asked the prosecutor if he had lost any thing; he saidNo, and asked his son where the two gold watches were - he said up stairs; a person brought in the knives, and then I was taken to the watch-house - the prosecutor there said he saw some person in his shop, and he thought it was his son exposing goods for sale; the Magistrate asked him if he saw me take the knives - he said No, but he saw my arm in the window; I asked him why he did not attack me at the moment - he said he thought it was his son; I asked him again, and he said he did not want to be asked any questions by me - the Magistrate said I must tell a Jury that story.
ANTHONY THOPSON re-examined. I believe he saw two persons in his shop and one ran one way and the other the other, and he thought one was his son - he first asked if the gold watches were safe; he did not then say any thing about his son.
THOMAS SAUNDERS re-examined. Q. How long was it from first to last? A. I suppose not three minutes.
Cross-examined. Q. Were your shutters up? A. Yes, but the door was open, and it was sufficiently light for me to see the prisoner.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18291029-214]
2057. JANE RIDLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of September, 1 blanket, value 5s., the goods of James Watt.
JAMES WATT. The prisoner had a room on the same floor as me. On the 12th of September my wife was out nursing, and I had to make my own bed - I went out, and put the key under the mat in case my wife should come home; when I came home I saw the bed clothes pulled about, and missed the blanket - there is no mark on this, but I had a pair of them, and this is the same as the other.
CHARLES WILLIAMSON. This blanket was pawned with me by the prisoner, on the 12th of September, about twelve or one o'clock.
Prisoner's Defence. That man has sworn false - I came home, took my own blanket, and pawned it for 2s.; he has owed me a great animosity for some time, because I said he brought in women of a night.
JAMES WATT re-examined. Q. Did she complain of you for bringing women to the house? A. No; I never had any one there but my wife; I have lived with Mr. Burgin eleven years - the man she lives with is not her husband; she came up to me for a light when they came there, and I said to my wife, "I don't like these people, for I heard him beat her, and she threatened to hang him;" I never spoke to her but once in anger, when she was coming up- I told her to keep her distance; I am sure my blanket was gone.
CHARLES WILLIAMSON re-examined. Q. Did she say before the Magistrate it was her own? A. Yes; she has not often pawned blankets, but I have known her these twenty years, and never knew any thing wrong of her.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18291029-215]
2068. ANN RICHARDS was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of October, 1 bolster, value 6d.; 1 blanket, value 6d.; 1 sheet, value 1s., and 3 yards of carpet, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Elizabeth Clark.
ELIZABETH CLARK. I am a widow. The prisoner lodged with me for three weeks, till the 18th of October, in Cow-lane, New Gravel-lane - it was a furnished room; I did not know she was going. On the 19th of October I took my child to school; when I returned there was no one in the house - I missed the articles stated.
CHRISTOPHER GREEN. I took the prisoner on the 19th of October - I found 12s. 8d. on her, and a duplicate of a bed pawned for 14s.
JAMES WAYLING. I am a Police-officer. The prisoner was brought to our office; I asked what had become of the things - she told me she had taken them to Bluegate-fields, and sold them; I went there, and found all but the bolster-case.
EDWARD EDMOND CHILD. I am a pawnbroker, and live in High-street, Shadwell. I have a bed, but the person who took it in is out of town; I have seen the prisoner at my master's.
Prisoner's Defence. I did not tell the officer I had sold them; I said I had left them for a few halfpence - she gave me leave to take any thing I liked, as she knew the distress I was in.
ELIZABETH CLARK. How could I do so? I never did.
JAMES WAYLING. She told me she left them at a house a Jew lives in, who I knew took in such things; I went and asked for them - they were delivered up.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 29.
Confined Six Weeks.

2059. WILLIAM SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of September, 1 jacket, value 5s.; 1 shilling, and 1 halfpenny, the property of Diedirick Klinck.
DIEDIRICK KLINCK. I am a German. On the 28th of September I was at the King Harry, in Red Lion-street, Whitechapel, about ten o'clock at night; there was fiddling up stairs - I went up; a man put his hand into my pocket, and took my money - I caught hold of him, and several of them beat me; the landlord came up, and took me down - I said I would not go without that man; they came down, and said I should fight, and they would give me fair play - I pulled off my jacket, and the prisoner was taken with it.
GEORGE HEARNE. I am a watchman. I produce the jacket which I took from the prisoner - this shilling and this halfpenny were in the pocket; I was going to cry ten o'clock, saw the row, and the prisoner was running from the crowd with it in his hand - he turned the corner and I caught him; I asked him how he came by it - he at first said it was his own, and then said it was the man's who was fighting, and he was going to take it home to his wife; I took him to the watch-house, then went to the crowd, and brought the prosecutor, who claimed it.
Prisoner's Defence. I found the jacket half a dozen doors from where they were fighting.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18291029-217]
2060. SARAH SHARP and ELIZABETH RYAN were indicted for stealing, on the 9th of October, 1 watch, value 70s., and 1 watch-chain, value 1s., the goods of Henry Brockman, from his person.
HENRY BROCKMAN. I am a porter. On the 9th ofOctober, about eight o'clock in the evening, I was in Long-lane; the two prisoners and another woman came up, and asked me to give them something to drink, which I did, at a wine-vaults, and we walked on to Chequer-ally - they then fell upon me, and Ryan took my watch from my fob; Sharpe held me while Ryan handed the watch to another, who ran away - I held Ryan till the watchman came, and took her; I am quite sure of their person - I was with them about half an hour.
Prisoner Ryan. Q. Were you not drinking with three men, in a court? A. There were two men with me, and we had one pot of beer; there was no woman with me - I did not say to you."I have treated this woman, and now I will treat you."
COURT. Q. Where were you going? A. I had a window sash to carry to Clerkenwell-green - they went with me there, and on my return robbed me.
Prisoner Ryan. Q. Did you not ask me to go to Clerkenwell, I refused, and you said you had no more money, till you had been there? A. No, I did not - I was not in any private house with a woman.
GEORGE HOOKER. I am a watchman. On the evening of the 9th of October, about half-past nine o'clock, I heard a cry of Watch! I went up to the place, and saw the prosecutor and Ryan together - he said he had lost his watch, and Ryan had robbed him of it; he said there three women had been, and they had collared him like a bull dog - I took Ryan to the watch-house.
THOMAS EARLE. I am headborough. I was at the watch-house, and saw Ryan brought in; I then went with the prosecutor and an officer, to the Salmon and Ball, Salmon and Ball-court, Bunhill-row - I said, "Look round, and see if there is any body you know; "he pointed out Sharpe - I took her into custody; she seemed to refuse to come, but I said, "You must come - the man says you are the person; "She then came - the prosecutor was sober.
EDWARD HANDS. I am a headborough. I was in a public-house three days after, and heard some girls say the chain was found by Britt - I knew him, and went and got it.
JAMES BRITT. I got this chain from my wife, and gave it to the officer.
ELLEN BRITT. I found this chain in Chequer-alley on the 10th of October; I shewed the place to the officer.
EDWARD HANDS. I went to the place where she said she found it, and it was close to the place where the prosecutor stated he was robbed.
Sharpe's Defence. I went to the Salmon and Ball, to get my husband home; he was out, and I waited - the officer and the prosecutor came in, and they said I must go with them.
Ryan's Defence. I was coming across Smithfield, and saw the prosecutor drinking with three men and a woman; he gave me some gin, and asked how far I was going - I said to Bunhill-row; he refused to go with me- I went home, then went out, and passed Chequer-alley, where I saw him; he took hold of me, and said I had robbed him.
ISABELLA SAWYER. I live at No. 2, Chequer-alley. I was with Ryan, on the 9th of October, from half-past seven o'clock, till she was taken - she was not out of my sight; we had taken a walk together, after I had done work she then went with me to my own room, and staid three quarters of an hour - we then went out again; I heard the cry of Watch! - I turned and saw the prosecutor with his small-clothes down, in a very indecent manner; he took hold of her, and said, "You have got my watch" - she said, "Nonsense man;" he let her go, and then took her again; I had not seen the prosecutor drinking.
JAMES CANNON. I am a labourer, and have lived at No. 4, Chequer-alley, about eight months; I never saw the prisoners, but the prosecutor came to my house, with a girl, as near as I can guess about half-past eight o'clock that night - I had just come home from my work, at Mr. Garrett's, an oil-man, in Whitecross-street; I had not been in two minutes, when the prosecutor came in, and went up stairs - he had not been there three minutes, when he came down again; the young woman then came down, and bade me good night - I said to her, "It is strange that a man should come to a house of this kind, and not pay for it;" I looked out, and he turned to the right hand - the girl went after him, and he followed her up; I went it, sat down, and in a few minutes, I heard Watch! cried - I went out, and saw the prosecutor running with his trousers down; he caught hold of Ryan, and said, "You have robbed me of my watch" - "No(says she) I have not;" "You d - d b - h, you have, (says he) and I have got hold of you;" I did not take any part in it, nor ask him for any thing for my room - I thought it very indecent for any man to do a thing of that kind.
GEORGE HOOKER re-examined. Q.How near were you, when the cry of Stop thief! was raised? A. About six doors; the prosecutor's clothes were up - Ryan said nothing about any indecent attack upon her; she only said she would go to the watch-house.
HENRY BROCKMAN. I did not go to any house with any young woman; my trousers were not down at all - I am married.
Prisoner Ryan. He is sure not to own it, because his wife is in Court.
SHARPE - GUILTY. Aged 21.
RYAN - GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18291029-218]
2061. CHARLOTTE STROUD was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of October, 44 sovereigns, and 6 half-sovereigns, the monies of Joseph Singer.
JOSEPH SINGER. I am a currier. On the 15th of October, I went to Drury-lane Theatre, with a friend; I got tipsy - Stephens, the officer, took me to some watch-house, and I left there fifty-nine sovereigns in a bag, in the care of the inspector of the Police - I then had three or four shillings in my pocket; I met the prisoner that night, went home and slept with her, at No. 7, Earl-street - the next day she went with me to the watch-house, and waited outside, while I got my sovereigns, and took some things out of pawn for her; we then went up to the Regent's-park, and to the Zoological-gardens - we had something to eat and drink, and came home to her house again; Mrs. Lloyd was with us - I sent for two bottles of sherry, and one of port; Mrs. Lloyd saw my money - it was in a bed-room; Lloyd went home about ten o'clock - I then locked the door; the prisoner and Iwent to bed - she had put something into the wine, that made me rather drowsy; I did not see her do it, but I think there must have been something - I put my trousers under my head, with forty-seven sovereigns in them; in the morning about half-past six o'clock, a person came and called Charlotte - that awoke me, and when I roused up, my money was all gone, and my trousers were on the bed; I recollect her lying down the night before, but whether her clothes were off, I do not know - I was quite sensible when I put my money into my trousers pocket, and when I went to bed.
Prisoner. Q. Can you say that I took the money from you? A. I can only say that I locked the door myself; no one could come in, and it was half open in the morning - there was a lock on the door; there had been a padlock on it, and I told the landlord, if he did not put a proper lock on, I would not stay.
SAMUEL STEPHENS. I am an officer. I saw the prosecutor one evening, tipsy at Drury-lane theatre; he stated he had a great many sovereigns - I took him down stairs, and gave him to two Police-constables.
SARAH LLOYD. My husband is a cabinet-maker. I went to the room where the prisoner and the prosecutor were; at half past eight o'clock that evening, the prisoner met me on the stairs, and asked me to go to her place; I did - there was some wine on the table; the prosecutor offered me a glass, and I drunk it - the prisoner then fetched a bottle of sherry; it was ten minutes past ten, when I came away - I saw the prosecutor have a bag of sovereigns, and he gave her half a sovereign, to fetch the wine; I said "You had better put your money into your bag, and take care of it" - I asked the prisoner her motive for giving him so much wine; she said, "That is to myself, Mrs. Lloyd; "I wished them good night, and when I came out, some one locked the door - the prisoner left me with him while she went for a bottle of wine- he gave me some wine to take to my husband, who was ill; I saw the prisoner offer the prosecutor a glass of wine, while he laid on the bed, which he refused for some time, but she urged it on him, and he took it - I saw the prisoner at Bow-street.
MARY CHERRY. The prisoner lodged in my house. I saw the prosecutor in her room on Friday; and on the following morning about two o'clock, I saw the prisoner, who said she wanted something to drink, and asked me if I thought she could get it - I said I did not know, without it was at the watering-house: she said she wanted change for a sovereign, and she thought she could not get it there - she went away from my room, and went along the passage with a candle; I did not see her again, but in the morning I saw the prosecutor go out with two Police-men - the prisoner left me, I think, last Saturday three weeks.
SARAH ALLINGTON. I live in Church-street, Bethnal-green - my husband is a dealer in marine-stores. At six o'clock in the morning, last Saturday three weeks, the prisoner came to my place - I had seen her but once before; she came in a cabriolet - she came to take farewell of me, and said she was going into the country to her friends; I saw some gold in a paper, but I do not know how much - she said it was money which had been left her by her brother; she asked my advice as to what she should do - I advised her to clothe herself genteely, and go to service; I went with her, and purchased a purse and a variety of other things - she stopped with me till last Monday fortnight, and then went put out to take a walk; while she was gone I heard a person had lost some money - I was afraid it was her, and advised her to leave my place at night.
SAMUEL STEPHENS. I took up the prisoner on Wednesday, the 21st of October, I believe - I found her in Shoreditch; I had heard of the robbery a few days before - I told her I was a Bow-street officer, and took her for taking the sovereigns from Singer; she was surprised, and said she knew nothing of it - I took her into a public-house, and found half a crown and some halfpence upon her; she said it was no interest to me to take her to Bow-street, and a friend of hers would let me have 5l. to let her go - I took her to the watch-house, and the next morning before the Magistrate; I asked what she had done with her luggage - she said it was gone to the Saracen's Head to go to Bristol, but from further inquiries I found her things at the Bell and Mackarel, Mileend-road; there was a bed, a blanket, a counterpane, some black stuff, some calico, a bonnet, and a veil which appeared new, or nearly so, a box, and two hat boxes - in the box was a phial with some stuff in it; I took it to a chemist's, and they said it was sugar of lead, and shewed me the effect of it on pewter - I only knew it was her box by what I was told.
Prisoner. Mrs. Allington's brother carried the things there - the stuff in the phial was for an inflammation; I could take you to the surgeon who gave it to me - Mrs. Allington knows who it was.
SARAH ALLINGTON. She went out one morning to get some medicine - my brother Philip went with her; I saw her bring in a small phial, and she took a tea-spoonful of the stuff - her box went from my place to the Bell and Mackarel.
WILLIAM LIVERMORE. I took some things by the prisoner's directions, but I cannot tell the day.
The prisoner handed in a written defence, simply declaring her innocence.
GUILTY (of stealing only.) Aged 25.
Transported for Seven Years.

2065. ABRAHAM DEATH was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of October, 1 time-piece, value 40s., the goods of Daniel Deacon, the elder, and others.
CHARLES TWIST. I am in the service of Daniel Deacon, the elder, and his two sons, at the White Horse, Cripplegate. On the 27th of October, I was told there was a time-piece missing from the budget of a waggon, which went from there on the Saturday, to go to Cambridge, and returned on the Monday; this is the timepiece - it has the name of Deacon on it.
JOHN ROBINSON. I am a waggoner. I came with the waggon from Buntingford last Monday - the time-piece was safe at six o'clock, and when we got to London I missed it; I took the prisoner up on the road out of charity, as he said he had no money, and brought him to Stamford-hill.
JOSEPH FOX. I am the guard of the waggon. The prisoner was taken up out of charity, and sat on the front of the waggon, by the time-piece, which hung in a leather budget in front.
GEORGE WILLMOTT. The prisoner and the time-piece were given to me by Fox - the prisoner cried, and seemed sorry.
JOSEPH FOX. The prisoner told us where he was apprentice - we went there, and found where his father lived; he was at home having his breakfast on the Tuesday morning - we called him to the door, and asked him for it; he denied it twice, and then said he had given it to his sister - he sent her for it, and gave it us.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.

2066. JOHN BEDFORD was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of October, 4 live tame fowls, price 10s., the property of William Williams.
WILLIAM WILLIAMS. I live at the Bell, at Ealing, and keep fowls - they were safe on Wednesday morning at seven o'clock, and in the evening at seven I missed four; I have known the prisoner ten years - he is a drover, and has passed and called at my house; he lives in London - I have seen my fowls since, and knew them; they were then dead.
GEORGE GODDARD. I was going across Ealing-common with two men that evening; we saw the prisoner lying on the common, and we called him - he got up, and walked on towards Acton; we saw something at the place he had been at, and went and found four fowls - the feathers were picked off one, and being white we saw them; I sent the two men after the prisoner - he said he found them there.
JOSEPH SMITH. I am a constable. The prisoner was put into my custody - I shewed the fowls to Mr. Williams, who owned them.
Prisoner's Defence. I had called at the prosecutor's house that morning, and had 1 1/2d. of gin; I was going across the common at half-past six o'clock, and there was something white; my dog saw it first, and put his nose close to me, as much as to say, "Master, come" - I went and found the fowls; I said, "They don't belong to me, "and I did not touch them - I was going on to Acton, and they took me.
GEORGE GODDARD re-examined. He was lying down on the ground, and when he got up we saw the fowls - it was rather dark; the fowl that was plucked seemed to have been done with the hand, and they were quite warm.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 40.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Two Months.

2067. JOHN SORLTER was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of September, 12 pieces of stained paper, value 3l. 16s., the goods of Jeffery Jolly.
JEFFERY JOLLY. I am a paper-stainer, and live in Marylebone. I have known the prisoner in the trade - he came to me on the 15th of September, and said he had a job for a gentleman who had sold his house, and must have it papered immediately, and wanted some patterns; I gave him some - he came the next day, and said the gentleman had selected one - I gave him to understand there would be no credit; I called one of my lads, and told him to take the papers to the gentleman, but not leave them without the money; I gave him a receipt - the prisoner said the gentleman's name was Sorlter, and his own name was John Brown.
WILLIAM COOPER. I was sent by the prosecutor on the 15th of September, with twelve pieces of paper; he told me not to leave them without the money - I went with the prisoner, and when we got to the house he said he had known Mr. Jolly many years, and he would call in the evening and pay the money; I left the paper in the kitchen.
JOHN GRINDLEY. I am a pawnbroker. I have three pieces of paper, pawned by the prisoner on the 16th of September, in the name of John Sorlter.
DANIEL DUTCH. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, and found three rooms had been papered with this paper, at No. 1, Grosvenor-place, Chelsea, where the witness took them, and these three pieces were pawned at Chelsea.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. He saw the wife of the person it was forcome up stairs with a child in her arms; here is the bill of parcels the prosecutor gave me - I was obliged to make away with the rest of it, as Mr. Kenyon was not at home.
JEFFERY JOLLY. This bill was made out for the gentleman he stated it was for - I went to the house, and the woman refused to tell me any thing about the prisoner - I understand he has been paid for it.
GUILTY. Aged 38.
Confined Four Months.
